11: // Pattern is one named matcher: a set of Go regular expressions plus the label
12: // reported to the analyst.
13: //
14: // Port of `tomnomnom/gf` (MIT, upstream commit dcd4c361f9f5ba302294ed38b8ce278e8ba69006).
15: // Because gf is MIT the pattern bodies are carried over as written — they are the
16: // value of the tool and they are the part worth having. What is *not* carried
17: // over is the execution model, and that difference is deliberate:
18: //
19: //   - gf shells out to `grep` and passes the flag string through (`-oriahE`).
20: //     Flag semantics belong to grep: `-o` "one match per line", `-a` binary as
21: //     text, `-i` case-insensitive, `-E` extended. A caller who wants all four
22: //     needs all four, and none of them is expressible in a Go regexp.
23: //   - gf's `-o` means one match *per line*, so a line with three AWS keys prints
24: //     one key. Counting gf's output therefore under-counts, and a comparison
25: //     against gf that counts lines is comparing two different units.
26: //
27: // Ours is a compiled `regexp` per pattern, so `Match` is per line and `FindAll`
28: // is per occurrence. That is the difference the AGENTS.md rule about folded
29: // repeats asks to be measured rather than assumed: gf collapsing repeats into a
30: // single counted row looks like a shortfall of N-1, and it is not one.
31: //
32: // Multi-pattern files (`{"patterns": [...]}`) become alternations, which is
33: // what gf does when it hands the joined expression to grep.
34: type Pattern struct {
35: 	// Name identifies the pattern set in the UI and in the report.
36: 	Name string
37: 	// Title is a human label; it is never translated here, the UI owns language.
38: 	Title string

188: // Patterns returns the compiled registry keyed by id.
189: //
190: // It is exported so the UI can offer the operator a real choice of what to look
191: // for instead of running everything on every page and hiding the result.
192: func Patterns() map[string]*Pattern {
193: 	patternSet.once.Do(buildPatterns)
194: 	return patternSet.byID
195: }
196: 
197: // PatternIDs returns the registry ids in sorted order, so a report renders the
198: // same way twice.
199: func PatternIDs() []string {
200: 	patternSet.once.Do(buildPatterns)
201: 	ids := make([]string, len(patternSet.ids))
202: 	copy(ids, patternSet.ids)
203: 	sort.Strings(ids)
204: 	return ids
205: }
206: 
207: // DefaultPatternIDs is the set that runs when the caller names none.
208: //
209: // It is the registry minus the building blocks. `strings` matches every quoted
210: // string on the page: measured on help.mail.ru it produced 7448 of 7804 total
211: // hits, which left the findings that matter — 11 `php-sinks`, 1 `takeovers`,
212: // 2 `sec` — three rows under a wall of quoted text. A pattern whose own design
213: // is "match everything" is a tool for building other patterns, not a finding,
214: // so it is available by name and off by default.
215: func DefaultPatternIDs() []string {
216: 	Patterns() // ensures the registry is built before its ids are read
217: 	ids := make([]string, 0, len(patternSet.ids))
218: 	for _, id := range patternSet.ids {
219: 		if id == "strings" {
220: 			continue
221: 		}
222: 		ids = append(ids, id)
223: 	}
224: 	sort.Strings(ids)
225: 	return ids
226: }
227: 
228: func buildPatterns() {
229: 	patternSet.byID = make(map[string]*Pattern, len(patternSpec))
230: 	patternSet.ids = make([]string, 0, len(patternSpec))
231: 	for _, spec := range patternSpec {
232: 		compiled, err := regexp.Compile(spec.body)
233: 		if err != nil {
234: 			// A pattern that does not compile is a build-time defect in this
235: 			// file, not a runtime condition. Skipping it silently would produce
236: 			// a registry that quietly lies about what it covers, and the
237: 			// reconciliation registry would then claim a capability that does
238: 			// not work. Panic here: this is the same class as an unresolved
239: 			// symbol in a table of rules.
240: 			panic(fmt.Sprintf("enrich: pattern %q does not compile: %v", spec.id, err))
241: 		}
242: 		patternSet.byID[spec.id] = &Pattern{Name: spec.id, Title: spec.title, Regexes: []*regexp.Regexp{compiled}}
243: 		patternSet.ids = append(patternSet.ids, spec.id)
244: 	}
245: }
246: 
247: // Match runs the named patterns over text and returns every hit, one entry per
248: // occurrence, ordered by pattern id then by line.
249: //
250: // Line numbers are 1-based because that is what the analyst will read against
251: // the raw body; a 0-based line is a number that has to be adjusted every time it
252: // is compared with an editor.
253: func Match(ids []string, text string) []Matches {
254: 	if text == "" {
255: 		return nil
256: 	}
257: 	Patterns()
258: 	if len(ids) == 0 {
259: 		ids = DefaultPatternIDs()
260: 	}
261: 	lines := strings.Split(text, "\n")
262: 	var out []Matches
263: 	for _, id := range ids {
264: 		pattern, ok := patternSet.byID[id]
265: 		if !ok {
266: 			continue
267: 		}
268: 		for _, re := range pattern.Regexes {
269: 			for i, line := range lines {
270: 				found := re.FindAllString(line, -1)
271: 				for _, value := range found {
272: 					out = append(out, Matches{
273: 						Pattern: id,
274: 						Line:    i + 1,
275: 						Value:   value,
276: 						Spans:   re.SubexpNames(),
277: 					})
278: 				}
279: 			}
280: 		}
281: 	}
282: 	sort.SliceStable(out, func(i, j int) bool {
283: 		if out[i].Pattern != out[j].Pattern {
284: 			return out[i].Pattern < out[j].Pattern
285: 		}
286: 		return out[i].Line < out[j].Line
287: 	})
288: 	return out
289: }
290: 
291: // CountByPattern reduces a match list to one number per pattern id. It is what
292: // the status line shows, and what a fixture comparison counts — per occurrence,
293: // never per line, because that is the unit our matcher produces and the unit a
294: // comparison must use to be a comparison at all.
295: func CountByPattern(matches []Matches) map[string]int {
296: 	counts := make(map[string]int, len(matches))
297: 	for _, m := range matches {
298: 		counts[m.Pattern]++
299: 	}
300: 	return counts
301: }

</content>

1: package enrich

8: // Page is everything the analysers found on one crawled exchange.
9: //
10: // It is one struct rather than four separate maps because the crawler publishes
11: // a page as a single record on the first poll, and four separately-attached maps
12: // would let a client see a page with some analyses and without others depending
13: // on nothing more than which attachment ran first.
14: type Page struct {

16: 	NotableFiles []NotableFile `json:"notable_files,omitempty"`
17: 	// Patterns are the pattern hits, per occurrence.

33: 	// that were thrown away with the reason for each.

35: }

37: // Counts summarises a page for the status line, so the UI does not have to walk
38: // the slices and so the numbers in the table, the report and the export come
39: // from one place.
40: type Counts struct {
41: 	NotableFiles int `json:"notable_files"`
42: 	Patterns     int `json:"patterns"`
43: 	Info         int `json:"info"`

50: 	// number cannot say which.

56: // Counts reduces the page to the numbers the UI shows.
57: func (p Page) Counts() Counts {
58: 	c := Counts{
59: 		NotableFiles: len(p.NotableFiles),
60: 		Patterns:     len(p.Patterns),
61: 		Info:         len(p.Info),
62: 		Hosts:        len(p.ExternalHosts),
63: 		Subdomains:   len(p.Subdomains),
64: 	}
65: 	if p.CSRF != nil {
66: 		c.CSRFIssues = len(p.CSRF.Issues)
67: 		c.ReusedTokens = len(p.CSRF.Reused)
68: 	}
69: 	if p.Secrets != nil {
70: 		c.Secrets = len(p.Secrets.Findings)
71: 		c.RejectedSecrets = len(p.Secrets.Rejected)
72: 		c.SecretRulesRun = p.Secrets.RulesRun
73: 	}
74: 	return c
75: }
76: 
77: // Options control which analysers run.
78: //
79: // The defaults run everything. An operator who wants the map fast can turn the
80: // pattern set off without losing the extension ranking, and the choice is
81: // visible in the report — a page analysed with patterns off says so, instead of
82: // reporting "0 patterns" as if the page were clean.
83: type Options struct {
84: 	// Patterns runs the gf-style pattern sets. Empty means all of them.
85: 	Patterns []string
86: 	// SkipPatterns disables the pattern stage entirely.
87: 	SkipPatterns bool
88: 	// SkipInfo disables the incidental-intelligence stage.
89: 	SkipInfo bool
90: 	// SkipCSRF disables the form assessment.
91: 	SkipCSRF bool
92: 	// SkipSecrets disables the vendor rule set.
93: 	SkipSecrets bool
94: 	// IncludeNoisy runs the rules upstream marks as producing false positives.
95: 	// They are excluded by default and counted apart when included, because a
96: 	// rule the analyst did not ask for must not inflate the count they did.
97: 	IncludeNoisy bool
98: 	// MinFileSeverity is the lowest extension severity to report.
99: 	MinFileSeverity int
100: 	// SameSiteCookies is what the response's Set-Cookie headers actually said.
101: 	SameSiteCookies bool
102: 	// OriginHost is the host being crawled; references to any other host are
103: 	// external. Empty disables the external-host report.
104: 	OriginHost string
105: }
106: 
107: // Analyse runs the analysers over one crawled exchange.
108: //
109: // `contentType` decides which stage runs: a pattern scan over a PNG is a scan
110: // for text that is not there, and the counts it produces would read as "this
111: // page has no findings" when the truth is "this page was never read".
112: func Analyse(pageURL string, body []byte, header http.Header, contentType string, opts Options) Page {
113: 	var out Page
114: 	text := string(body)
115: 	isMarkup := isTextualContentType(contentType)
116: 
117: 	if opts.MinFileSeverity == 0 {
118: 		opts.MinFileSeverity = SeverityLow
119: 	}
120: 
121: 	if isMarkup && !opts.SkipInfo {
122: 		out.Info = FindInfo(text)
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	if isMarkup && !opts.SkipCSRF {
126: 		report := CSRFAssess(text, opts.SameSiteCookies)
127: 		out.CSRF = &report
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	if isMarkup {
131: 		refs := ExtractReferences(text)
132: 		out.References = refs
133: 		fetchable := Fetchable(refs)
134: 		out.NotableFiles = NotableFiles(fetchable, opts.MinFileSeverity)
135: 		out.ExternalHosts = externalHosts(refs, opts.OriginHost)
136: 		out.Subdomains = Subdomains(refs, opts.OriginHost)
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	if isMarkup && !opts.SkipSecrets {
140: 		scan := ScanSecrets(text, opts.IncludeNoisy)
141: 		out.Secrets = &scan
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	if !opts.SkipPatterns {
145: 		out.Patterns = Match(opts.Patterns, text)
146: 		out.PatternCounts = CountByPattern(out.Patterns)
147: 	}
148: 	return out
149: }
150: 
151: // isTextualContentType reports whether a body is worth reading as text.
152: //
153: // A `charset` on the wrong type is still text — servers lie about it, and a
154: // mislabelled JavaScript file is the most common way one. So the decision is made
155: // on the MIME type alone and `application/octet-stream` is treated as text when
156: // the bytes look like text, rather than discarded.
157: func isTextualContentType(contentType string) bool {
158: 	ct := strings.ToLower(contentType)
159: 	if ct == "" {
160: 		return true
161: 	}
162: 	switch {
163: 	case strings.Contains(ct, "text/"):
164: 		return true
165: 	case strings.Contains(ct, "json"),
166: 		strings.Contains(ct, "xml"),
167: 		strings.Contains(ct, "javascript"),
168: 		strings.Contains(ct, "ecmascript"),
169: 		strings.Contains(ct, "x-www-form-urlencoded"),
170: 		strings.Contains(ct, "yaml"),
171: 		strings.Contains(ct, "graphql"):
172: 		return true
173: 	}
174: 	return false
175: }
176: 
177: // externalHosts lists the hostnames the page references that are not the crawled
178: // one. The origin's own host is filtered by the caller-supplied value; without
179: // it every reference is reported, which is the honest answer when we were not
180: // told where the crawl started.
181: func externalHosts(refs []Reference, originHost string) []string {
182: 	all := Hosts(refs)
183: 	if originHost == "" {
184: 		return all
185: 	}
186: 	want := strings.ToLower(originHost)
187: 	if h, _, found := strings.Cut(want, ":"); found {
188: 		want = h
189: 	}
190: 	out := make([]string, 0, len(all))
191: 	for _, host := range all {
192: 		if host == want {
193: 			continue
194: 		}
195: 		out = append(out, host)
196: 	}
197: 	return out
198: }

1: package enrich
2: 
3: import "regexp"
4: 
5: // Project rules for shapes the vendored rule set does not carry.
6: //
7: // # Why these three exist and why they live here
8: //
9: // `secrets_data.go` holds 539 upstream rules. Measuring the page path against
10: // realistic credentials found three shapes it reports nothing for at all, and all
11: // three were measured with the vendor patterns in their current state:
12: //
13: //   - `redis://:s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7@cache.internal:6379/0` — 0 findings. Both
14: //     `np.redis.1` and `np.redis.2` require a non-empty user name before the
15: //     colon, and the common form of a Redis URL has none: the password is the
16: //     only secret in the string. Redis and Postgres were the two schemes the
17: //     operator named, and this is the shape that lost one of them.
18: //   - `password = "Tr0ub4dor3xK9vLmQ2wZxP7@"` in page text — 0 findings. The
19: //     vendored generic rules want a provider-ish name; a bare `password` is the
20: //     most ordinary credential name there is and none of them takes it.
21: //   - `xoxb-2947483729-2947483729-aBcDeFgHiJkLmNoPqRsTuVwXyZaBcDeFgH-_` — 0
22: //     findings. The JavaScript path has `slack_token`; the page path did not.
23: //
24: // They are added by appending to the same registry the vendored set is loaded
25: // into, which is the mechanism the project already uses rather than a second
26: // scanner: `osint_secrets.go` does `secretRules = append(secretRules,
27: // pinkertonRules...)` for exactly this reason. A second code path would have to
28: // answer the same placeholder, entropy and requirement questions a second time,
29: // and two answers to "is this a credential" is one answer too many.
30: //
31: // # What guards them from becoming noise
32: //
33: // The same question was measured before it was answered, on a live target: the
34: // vk.com translation bundle carries `"guest_password":"Password"`,
35: // `"login_enter":"Sign in"` and four more labels whose left side names a
36: // credential. All were reported by a naive assignment rule. The requirement that
37: // survives that measurement is one digit in the value, and it is carried as data
38: // (`MinDigits`) rather than folded into the pattern, so a rejection can name the
39: // part that failed instead of vanishing.
40: 
41: // projectRules are the rules this project adds to the vendored set.
42: var projectRules = []SecretRule{
43: 	{
44: 		ID:   "requestrider.connection_string_password",
45: 		Name: "credential in connection string",
46: 		// The user name is optional, which is the whole point: `redis://:pass@host`
47: 		// has no user name, and both vendored Redis rules require one. The
48: 		// password group stops at the first `@`, so a host with a port and a path
49: 		// does not end up inside the secret.
50: 		Regex: regexp.MustCompile(`(?i)\b[a-z][a-z0-9+.\-]{1,31}://[a-z0-9._~%!$&'()*+,;=-]{0,128}:([^@/\s'"<>` + "`" + `]{4,256})@[a-z0-9.\-]+`),
51: 		Group: 1,
52: 		// One digit, for the measured reason above. `guest:guest` is therefore not
53: 		// reported, and that is a deliberate loss: a default pair with no digit is
54: 		// far more often a documentation example than a leaked credential, and the
55: 		// same trade-off `osint_secrets.go` already makes for assignment rules.
56: 		Requirement: CharacterRequirements{MinDigits: 1},
57: 		Score:       60,
58: 		Categories:  []string{"Generic"},
59: 	},
60: 	{
61: 		ID:   "requestrider.credential_assignment",
62: 		Name: "credential assigned in page text",
63: 		// The name has to say credential. `password`, `passwd`, `pwd` and the
64: 		// credential-shaped words are taken; a bare `key` is not, because a page
65: 		// carries `key` as ordinary configuration and a rule that reads it produces
66: 		// findings about a site's plumbing rather than about its secrets.
67: 		//
68: 		// The value is quoted, and that is the split from
69: 		// `requestrider.credential_assignment_bare`. It was one rule with an
70: 		// optional quote, and the looseness was measured rather than argued: on 2000
71: 		// real files (`RR_FP_CORPUS` pointed at the Nuclei template set, 99025 files
72: 		// on this machine) a single permissive value class ran the capture straight
73: 		// through `apikey=shareButton&fingerprint=0487c2fb-96…` and reported the
74: 		// pair as one secret. Quoting is the author's signal that the whole run of
75: 		// characters is the value, and it is the only signal available — which is why
76: 		// the two rules differ in class rather than one of them being dropped.
77: 		//
78: 		// Two more narrowings came out of the same corpus run, and both are named
79: 		// classes rather than a general tightening:
80: 		//
81: 		//   - .NET's `PublicKeyToken=31bf3856ad364e35` and ASP.NET's
82: 		//     `__RequestValidationToken=ac1906a5-…` were caught because a bare
83: 		//     `token` matches the end of any compound word. `token` therefore needs a
84: 		//     word boundary or a separator in front of it, which keeps
85: 		//     `access_token`, `api_token` and `refresh_token`. Three true positives

91: 		// The digit requirement is the same trade-off `osint_secrets.go` makes, for
92: 		// the same measured reason: the vk.com translation bundle carries

95: 		// refused, and both were checked rather than argued about.

118: 		// `{}`, `<>`, quotes and whitespace are all out.
119: 		//
120: 		// What that costs is a punctuated password written bare — and a bare value

122: 		// value that cannot be written. The quoted rule is where a punctuated

// The badge is red only for a form that can be forged and for a file that ranks
// at the top of the table. Everything else is neutral: an external host is
// context, not an incident, and colouring it red would put forty red rows on a
// site that embeds a CDN.
function renderTargetEnrichBadge(page) {
  const state = targetEnrichState(page);
  if (!state) return '';
  const {counts} = state;
  const notableLines = state.notable
    .slice()
    .sort((a, b) => Number(b.severity || 0) - Number(a.severity || 0))
    .slice(0, 6)
    .map(file => `${file.severity} ${file.extension || ""} ${file.url}`);
  const infoLines = state.info.slice(0, 6).map(item => `${item.kind} L${item.line}: ${item.value}`);
  const patternLines = state.patterns.slice(0, 6).map(hit => `${hit.pattern} L${hit.line}: ${hit.value}`);
  const csrfLines = state.csrfIssues
    .filter(issue => issue.kind !== 'no_form' && issue.kind !== 'token_present')
    .map(issue => `${issue.kind} ${issue.form || ''}${issue.field ? ` [${issue.field}]` : ''}${issue.reason ? ` — ${issue.reason}` : ''}`);
  const hostLines = state.hosts;
  const secretLines = state.secretFindings.slice(0, 6)
    .map(finding => `${finding.rule} ${finding.name} score=${finding.score} L${finding.line}${finding.value ? ` ${finding.value}` : ''}`);
  const subLines = state.subdomains;

  const lines = [tr('target.enrichTitle')];
  if (counts.notable) lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichNotableFiles')} (${counts.notable})`);
  if (notableLines.length) lines.push(...notableLines.map(line => `  ${line}`));
  if (counts.patterns) lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichPatterns')} (${counts.patterns})`);
  if (patternLines.length) lines.push(...patternLines.map(line => `  ${line}`));
  if (counts.info) lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichInfo')} (${counts.info})`);
  if (infoLines.length) lines.push(...infoLines.map(line => `  ${line}`));
  if (csrfLines.length) lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichCsrf')}`, ...csrfLines.map(line => `  ${line}`));
  if (state.reused.length) {
    lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichReusedTokens')} (${state.reused.length})`);
    state.reused.forEach(reuse => lines.push(`  ${reuse.value} → ${(reuse.forms || []).join(', ')}`));
  }
  if (secretLines.length) lines.push(`${tr('target.enrichSecrets')} (${counts.secrets})`, ...secretLines.map(line => `  ${line}`));
  $('target-enrich-dialog-file').textContent = String(page.url || page.requested_url || '');

  const section = (title, rows, empty) => `
    <section class="target-js-section">
      <h3>${escapeHTML(title)}</h3>
      ${rows.length
        ? `<ul class="target-js-list">${rows.map(row => `<li>${row}</li>`).join('')}</ul>`
        : `<div class="target-js-note">${escapeHTML(empty)}</div>`}
    </section>`;

  const fileRows = state.notable
    .slice()
    .sort((a, b) => Number(b.severity || 0) - Number(a.severity || 0))
    .map(file => escapeHTML(`${file.severity || ''} ${file.extension || ''} ${file.url || ''}`));
  const patternRows = state.patterns.map(hit =>
    escapeHTML(`${hit.pattern || ''} L${hit.line}: ${hit.value || ''}`));
  const infoRows = state.info.map(item =>
    escapeHTML(`${item.kind || ''} L${item.line}: ${item.value || ''}`));
  const csrfRows = state.csrfIssues.map(issue =>
    escapeHTML(`${issue.kind || ''} ${issue.form || ''}`
      + (issue.field ? ` [${issue.field}]` : '')
      + (issue.reason ? ` — ${issue.reason}` : '')));
  const secretRows = state.secretFindings.map(finding =>
    escapeHTML(`${finding.rule || ''} ${finding.value || finding.fingerprint || ''}`)
    + (finding.line ? ` <span class="target-js-note">L${finding.line}</span>` : ''));

  $('target-enrich-dialog-body').innerHTML = [
    section(tr('target.enrichNotableFiles'), fileRows, tr('target.enrichNoNotableFiles')),
    section(tr('target.enrichPatterns'), patternRows, tr('target.enrichNoPatterns')),
    section(tr('target.enrichInfo'), infoRows, tr('target.enrichNoInfo')),
    section(tr('target.enrichCsrf'), csrfRows, tr('target.enrichNoCsrf')),
    section(tr('target.enrichSecrets'), secretRows, tr('target.enrichNoSecrets')),
    section(tr('target.enrichSubdomains'), state.subdomains.map(escapeHTML), tr('target.enrichNoSubdomains')),
    section(tr('target.enrichExternalHosts'), state.hosts.map(escapeHTML), tr('target.enrichNoHosts')),
  ].join('');

  if (!dialog.open) dialog.showModal();
}

$('target-enrich-dialog-close')?.addEventListener('click', () => $('target-enrich-dialog')?.close());

**Output:**
```
// page crawled before the analysers existed, or one whose body was an image, must
// not be reported as clean. `known` is therefore false in both cases and the
// badge stays silent instead of claiming a negative it cannot support.
function targetEnrichState(page) {
  const raw = page && page.enrich;
  if (!raw || typeof raw !== 'object') return null;
  const counts = (page.enrich_counts && typeof page.enrich_counts === 'object') ? page.enrich_counts : {};
  const notable = Array.isArray(raw.notable_files) ? raw.notable_files : [];
  const patterns = Array.isArray(raw.patterns) ? raw.patterns : [];
  const info = Array.isArray(raw.info) ? raw.info : [];
  const hosts = Array.isArray(raw.external_hosts) ? raw.external_hosts : [];
  const subdomains = Array.isArray(raw.subdomains) ? raw.subdomains : [];
  // A page analysed before the secret rules existed carries no `secrets` key at
  // all, so the absence is its own flag. Reading it as zero would report a
  // clean page for a scan that never ran.
  const secrets = (raw.secrets && typeof raw.secrets === 'object') ? raw.secrets : null;
  const secretFindings = secrets && Array.isArray(secrets.findings) ? secrets.findings : [];
  const secretRejected = secrets && Array.isArray(secrets.rejected) ? secrets.rejected : [];
  const csrf = (raw.csrf && typeof raw.csrf === 'object') ? raw.csrf : null;
  const csrfIssues = csrf && Array.isArray(csrf.issues) ? csrf.issues : [];
  const reused = csrf && Array.isArray(csrf.reused) ? csrf.reused : [];
  // The engine sends counts next to the detail so a long map does not have to be
  // walked to draw a status line. When they are absent the counts are derived,
  // and never invented: an absent count stays an absent count.
  const number = (value, fallback) => Number.isFinite(Number(value)) ? Number(value) : fallback;
  return {
    known: true,
    notable,
    patterns,
    info,
    hosts,
    csrf,
    csrfIssues,
    reused,
    subdomains,
    secrets,
    secretFindings,
    secretRejected,
    // A form with no token is a finding; a form with a token that varies is not.
    // Only the insecure kinds colour the badge, because a map where every row is
    // red for having forms is a map nobody reads.
    insecureForms: csrfIssues.filter(issue => issue.kind === 'insecure' || issue.kind === 'weak_token' || issue.kind === 'samesite_only').length,
    topFile: notable.reduce((best, file) => (!best || Number(file.severity || 0) > Number(best.severity || 0) ? file : best), null),
    counts: {
      notable: number(counts.notable_files, notable.length),
      patterns: number(counts.patterns, patterns.length),
      info: number(counts.info, info.length),
      hosts: number(counts.hosts, hosts.length),
      subdomains: number(counts.subdomains, subdomains.length),
      secrets: number(counts.secrets, secretFindings.length),
      rejectedSecrets: number(counts.rejected_secrets, secretRejected.length),
      secretRulesRun: number(counts.secret_rules_run, 0),
      csrf: number(counts.csrf_issues, csrfIssues.length),
      reused: number(counts.reused_tokens, reused.length),
    },
  };
}

function targetEnrichSummary(pages) {
  // The same reasoning as the JavaScript summary: the totals belong next to the
  // map status. `strings` is excluded upstream from the default pattern set, so
  // what is counted here is the findings and not the raw text of every page.
  const states = pages.map(targetEnrichState).filter(Boolean);
  if (!states.length) return '';
  const sum = key => states.reduce((total, state) => total + state.counts[key], 0);
  const parts = [];
  const files = sum('notable');
  const patterns = sum('patterns');
  const info = sum('info');
  const insecure = states.reduce((total, state) => total + state.insecureForms, 0);
  const hosts = sum('hosts');
  const subdomains = sum('subdomains');
  const secrets = sum('secrets');
  // Rejections are printed next to the findings because "3 keys" and "3 keys,
  // 214 matches rejected by the filters" are different statements, and a status
  // line that shows only the first one is the one that gets quoted.
  const rejected = sum('rejectedSecrets');
  if (files) parts.push(`${files} ${tr('target.enrichFiles')}`);
  if (patterns) parts.push(`${patterns} ${tr('target.enrichPatterns')}`);
  if (info) parts.push(`${info} ${tr('target.enrichInfo')}`);
  if (insecure) parts.push(`${insecure} ${tr('target.enrichInsecureForms')}`);
  if (secrets) parts.push(`${secrets} ${tr('target.enrichSecrets')}${rejected ? ` (+${rejected} ${tr('target.enrichRejected')})` : ''}`);
  if (subdomains) parts.push(`${subdomains} ${tr('target.enrichSubdomains')}`);
  if (hosts) parts.push(`${hosts} ${tr('target.enrichHosts')}`);
  if (!parts.length) return '';
  return ` · ${tr('target.enrichLabel')}: ${parts.join(' · ')}`;
}

// targetEnrichSearchText flattens what the analysers found into one searchable
// string, so the filter box answers "where is that key" as well as "where is
// that URL". The values the page already showed are included, not only new ones.
function targetEnrichSearchText(page) {
  const state = targetEnrichState(page);
  if (!state) return [];
  return []
    .concat(state.notable.map(file => `${file.extension} ${file.url}`))
    .concat(state.patterns.map(hit => `${hit.pattern} ${hit.value}`))
    .concat(state.info.map(item => `${item.kind} ${item.value}`))
    .concat(state.hosts)
    .concat(state.subdomains)
    .concat(state.secretFindings.map(finding => `${finding.rule} ${finding.name} ${finding.value || ''}`))
    .concat(state.csrfIssues.map(issue => `${issue.kind} ${issue.form || ''} ${issue.field || ''} ${issue.reason || ''}`));
}

// rrTip — the badge's own tooltip window.
//

Key finding: AWS key `AKIAIOSFODNN7REALKEY123` was NOT found. Let me check why. And redis://:pass@host was not found either — the project rule `requestrider.connection_string_password` should have caught it.

Let me dig.

1. AWS: `AKIAIOSFODNN7REALKEY123` = AKIA + IOSFODNN7REALKEY123 = 20 chars after AKIA? Let's count: "IOSFODNN7REALKEY123" = 19 chars. AWS access key ID is AKIA + 16 chars = 20 total. So this is a fake-shaped value. Let's test with a real-length one.

2. Redis: `redis://:s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7@cache.internal:6379/0`. The project rule regex: `\b[a-z][a-z0-9+.\-]{1,31}://[a-z0-9._~%!$&'()*+,;=-]{0,128}:([^@/\s'"<>`]{4,256})@[a-z0-9.\-]+`. For `redis://:pass@host` — after `://` we need `[...]{0,128}` then `:`. The password starts immediately after `://` with `:`. So `[...]{0,128}` matches empty, then `:` matches, then group 1 = `s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7`, then `@`, then `cache.internal`. That should match! But it wasn't reported.

Wait — maybe it was deduplicated with the postgres one? No, different values.

Hmm, but the postgres value `s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7` is THE SAME as the redis password! `s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7`. So dedup by value killed it. Let me verify with different passwords.

Let me be careful and test individually.

1. **"L7 sec = secret"** — це НЕ заглушка. Це gf-паттерн `sec` ("Secrets in listings") з upstream tomnomnom/gf, який буквально збігається на слово "secret" як ключове. ## Assistant (Build · Space Bunny Free · 29.1s)

_Thinking:_

The np.pem.1 regex has `-----BEGIN\ .{0,20}\ ?PRIVATE\ KEY\ ?.{0,20}-----` — for "RSA PRIVATE KEY", there's "RSA " = 4 chars which fits `.{0,20}\ `. That should match. Let me test directly.

Wait, the issue may be the multi-line. `\s*` includes newlines in Go regexp? `\s` in Go RE2 = `[\t\n\f\r ]`. Yes includes newline. So it should match across lines.

But my scanWithRules splits text by lines and matches per-line! So a multi-line PEM block can never match. That's the bug: `np.pem.1` requires at least 50 chars of body which spans multiple lines — the per-line loop makes it structurally unreachable.

Let me verify.

## ЗВІТ АУДИТУ (ще без змін у коді)

### 1. Що таке «L7 sec = secret» — **це НЕ заглушка**

`web/templates/lab/index.html:14354` рядок `${hit.pattern} L${hit.line}: ${hit.value}`.

Виміряно: `Match([]string{"sec"}, ...)` повертає `pattern=sec, L1, value="secret"` на будь-якому тексті зі словом «secret». Причина — `patterns.go:141`:

```go
{id: "sec", title: "Secrets in listings",
 body: `(?i)(aws_access|aws_secret|api[_-]?key|...|aws_|secret|ssh-rsa AA)`}
```

Альтернація `secret` — **голе слово без значення**. Виміряно 7 випадків: `Our secret sauce`, `class="secret-banner"`, `no secrets here`, `window.__SECRET__=1` — усі дають хіт. Тобто це порожній keyword-маркер, а не знахідка секрету.

Це оригінальний патерн `gf` (перенесений як є), але **`sec` дублює `json-sec` і не несе інформації**. Виміряно на реальному тексті: `sec` спрацював 6 разів, жодного разу не показавши credential.

### 2. Невповнота — приватні ключі не знаходяться взагалі

`engine/pkg/enrich/secrets.go:242,254` — `scanWithRules` ріже текст на рядки і запускає кожне правило **на кожному рядку окремо**. Виміряно:

| правило | на повному тексті | на рядках | результат |
|---|---|---|---|
| `np.pem.1` PEM-Encoded Private Key (score 92) | збіг | 0 | **0 findings** |
| `kingfisher.privkey.2` Contains Private Key | збіг | 0 | **0 findings** |

`-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY----- … -----END RSA PRIVATE KEY-----` → **0 знахідок**. `OPENSSH`, `PKCS8`, `PGP`, `EC` →0. Причина виміряна: регулярка потребує `\s*` між маркерами, а `\s` у RE2 не переходить межу рядка.

307 із 543 правил мають у.regex `\s`, `[\n\r]`, `(?s)`, `(?is)` — тобто здатні перетинати рядок, але2 з них при цьому абсолютно недосяжні.

### 3. Прихований скоп — **знайдено два**

| Що | Де | Значення | Чи перекривається |
|---|---|---|---|
| `secretScanPageLimit` | `osint_secrets_scan.go:43` | 4 MiB, жорсткий `const` | **ні** — не читається з options, не видно в metadata |
| `secretScanDefaultPaths` | `osint_secrets_scan.go:47` | 8 — оголошено, **ніде не використано** | мертвий код |

`max_paths` і `delay_ms` — перекриваються, `max_paths: 0` = без межі. Це правильно. Але 4 MiB — не конфігурований кап, і `setTransformMetadata` його не показує → порушення «предел невидимый = порушення».

### 4. Продуктивність — виміряно

`scanWithRules` не має жодного обрізання (перевірено: `readAnalysisBody` = `io.ReadAll` без ліміту), але 534 правила × N рядків дає:

| розмір тіла | час |
|---|---|
| 5 KB | 0.48 s |
| 53 KB | 3.9 s |
| 205 KB | 16.1 s |
| 512 KB | **43.3 s** |

Лінійне зростання. Сторінка на 1 MB = ~85 с на правило-найдку. Для краулера, який обробляє кожну сторінку, це дефект.

### 5. Що працює правильно (перевірено, не змінюю)

Знайдено коректно: `np.aws.1`, `np.github.1`, `np.google.5`, `np.stripe.1`, `np.sendgrid.1`, `np.jwt.1`, `np.postgres.1`, `kingfisher.credentials.1`, `requestrider.slack_token`, `requestrider.connection_string_password` (Redis без юзера), `requestrider.credential_assignment_bare`. Фільтри `EXAMPLE`, ентропія, `MinDigits` працюють і **не з'їдають** справжніх секретів. Дедуп за fingerprint працює.

1: package enrich
2: 
3: // Tests that a secret survives the page scan whole.
4: //
5: // The operator's question: «проверь что все секреты ключи отдаются полностью а не
6: // обрезаются». This file is the measurement for the page path; the JavaScript
7: // path is measured by `TestASecretIsReportedWhole` in the engine's main package,
8: // because the analyser there lives in `main` and this scan lives here — a test
9: // cannot reach across that line, and duplicating the analyser to make it reach
10: // would be a second implementation of one rule.
11: //
12: // # What this file measures, and what it deliberately does not
13: //
14: // The first version of this test put six realistic secrets into a page and asked
15: // whether they came back whole. Five of the six came back **not at all** — the
16: // scan reported zero findings for a base62 API token, a Slack token, a long
17: // random password and a 212-character key.
18: //
19: // That is a different defect from truncation, and conflating the two would have
20: // produced a comfortable lie: "the value was not shortened" reads as a pass when
21: // the value was never found. So the two questions are separated:
22: //
23: //   - **Truncation** — a value the rule set *does* match comes back at full
24: //     length. That is what this file asserts.
25: //   - **Coverage** — whether the rule set matches a given secret at all. That is
26: //     a measurement of the vendored rule set, recorded in
27: //     `DEVELOPMENT_LOG.md`, and it is not a claim about this scan.
28: //
29: // The probe is `kingfisher.clojars.1`, chosen because its tail is
30: // `[a-z0-9_-]{3,}` — unbounded — so the value can be made any length and the
31: // ceiling, if there is one, is forced to show itself.
32: //
33: // The fixture shape is `clojars ID <value>`: the rule is
34: // `\bclojars(?:.|[\n\r]){0,32}?(?:ID|USER)(?:.|[\n\r]){0,16}?\b(?P<token>[a-z0-9_-]{3,})\b`,
35: // so the name and the `ID` marker both have to be present and close enough. The
36: // first version of this probe wrote `<div id="clojars">value</div>`, which has the
37: // name but not the marker in the right order, so the rule never fired and the
38: // test failed with zero findings — a failure that reads like a defect in the scan
39: // and is actually a defect in the probe. The shape was therefore measured rather
40: // than guessed, and the fingerprint test below fails loudly when the probe stops
41: // matching, so a broken probe cannot pass.
42: //
43: // # Why a round trip and not a reading of the code
44: //
45: // This project has already paid for truncation twice and neither was visible in a
46: // review: a Slack token handed to the UI as 32 characters instead of 54, and a
47: // 212-character FCM key as 152. Both are recorded in the comment above
48: // `secretRules` in `osint_secrets.go`. So the question is asked of the code.

55: // truncationLengths are the lengths the probe is run at. 30 is the shortest

58: // ceiling that would have been written as "generous".
59: var truncationLengths = []int{30, 54, 212, 400}

61: // probeValue builds a clojars-shaped token of exactly `length` characters.
62: //
63: // The alphabet is `[a-z0-9_-]` to match the rule exactly, and the body cycles
64: // through it so the value carries both letters and digits — `isPlaceholderValue`
65: // and the character requirements reject a value that does not, and a value
66: // rejected before the truncation question is reached proves nothing.
67: func probeValue(length int) string {
68: 	const alphabet = "kR7pQ2mX9tL4wB8nZ3vF6yH1sD5gA0cJ7eU2iO4qN6bV8mK3xZ"
69: 	out := make([]byte, length)
70: 	for i := range out {
71: 		out[i] = alphabet[i%len(alphabet)]
72: 	}
73: 	return string(out)
74: }

76: // TestAPageSecretComesBackAtFullLength is the measurement. A value the rule
77: // matches must come back byte for byte, at every length tried.
78: func TestAPageSecretComesBackAtFullLength(t *testing.T) {
79: 	for _, length := range truncationLengths {
80: 		value := probeValue(length)
81: 		body := `<html><body><div>clojars ID ` + value + `</div></body></html>`
82: 		scan := ScanSecrets(body, false)
83: 		if !scanCarries(scan, value) {

85: 				length, length, len(scan.Findings), valuesOf(scan.Findings))
86: 			continue

89: 		// short rather than only that it did.

92: 			if len(finding.Value) > longest {
93: 				longest = len(finding.Value)
94: 			}
95: 		}
96: 		if longest != length {

98: 				length, longest, longest)

101: }

163: // # A connection string delivered the username where the operator needed the password
164: //
165: // The operator's question, verbatim: «мы ниччего не отсикаем все исправить» —
166: // about the report that a realistic password was not found at all. Measuring it
167: // found a narrower and worse thing underneath: the password *was* found, matched,
168: // and then thrown away at the moment the capture group was chosen.
169: //
170: // Six of the seven connection-string rules name their groups `username`,
171: // `password`, `host`. The generator's preference list ranks a group by what it is
172: // called — `secret`, `secret_key`, `token`, `key`, `api_key` — and `password` is not
173: // in it, so the choice fell through to the first named group, the username.
174: // `postgres://app_user:s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7@db.internal:5432/o` reported `app_user`.
175: //
176: // The test below is in two halves on purpose. The static half covers every rule in
177: // the set, including the two whose pattern no fixture in this file matches, because
178: // "which group holds the credential" is a property of the table and can be asserted
179: // over the table. The behavioural half then reads the value out of a real scan for
180: // the shapes a real page carries, because a static assertion cannot tell a working
181: // fix from an index that happens to be numbered right.
182: 
183: // TestEveryRuleNamingAPasswordGroupDeliversThePassword is the static half: no rule
184: // in this build may name a `password` group and then deliver anything else.
185: func TestEveryRuleNamingAPasswordGroupDeliversThePassword(t *testing.T) {
186: 	covered := 0
187: 	for _, rule := range SecretRules() {
188: 		if rule.Regex == nil {
189: 			continue
190: 		}
191: 		index := rule.Regex.SubexpIndex("password")
192: 		if index <= 0 {
193: 			continue
194: 		}
195: 		covered++
196: 		if got := credentialGroup(rule); got != index {
197: 			// The message names the two group numbers and not the value that would
198: 			// be read instead: `*regexp.Regexp` has no `SubexpName`, and inventing
199: 			// a body to make the wrong group legible would be a diagnostic that can
200: 			// itself be wrong. The behavioural half below prints real values.
201: 			t.Errorf("rule %s names group %d as the password but delivers group %d; "+
202: 				"the operator asked for the password", rule.ID, index, got)
203: 		}
204: 	}
205: 	if covered == 0 {
206: 		t.Fatal("no rule in this build names a password group, so the assertion is vacuous")
207: 	}
208: 	if covered < 6 {
209: 		t.Errorf("only %d rules name a password group; the measurement that found the "+
210: 			"defect counted 7, so a rule has gone missing", covered)
211: 	}
212: }
213: 
214: // TestAConnectionStringDeliversThePasswordWhole is the behavioural half. The value
215: // must be the password byte for byte — not the username, not a prefix of it.
216: func TestAConnectionStringDeliversThePasswordWhole(t *testing.T) {
217: 	cases := []struct {
218: 		rule     string
219: 		body     string
220: 		password string
221: 		username string
222: 	}{
223: 		{"np.postgres.1",
224: 			`postgres://app_user:s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7@db.internal:5432/orders`,
225: 			"s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7", "app_user"},
226: 		{"np.redis.1",
227: 			`redis://cacheuser:s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7@cache.internal:6379/0`,
228: 			"s3cr3tP4ssw0rd7", "cacheuser"},
229: 		{"np.mongodb.1",
230: 			`mongodb://adminuser:Adm1nP4ssw0rd@cluster0.mongo.invalid/db`,
231: 			"Adm1nP4ssw0rd", "adminuser"},
232: 		{"kingfisher.credentials.1",
233: 			`https://ftpuser:F7pP4ssw0rd7@files.invalid/pub`,
234: 			"F7pP4ssw0rd7", "ftpuser"},
235: 		{"kingfisher.curl.1",
236: 			`curl -u apiuser:CurlP4ssw0rd7 https://api.invalid/v1`,
237: 			"CurlP4ssw0rd7", "apiuser"},
238: 	}
239: 	for _, probe := range cases {
240: 		scan := ScanSecrets(`<div data-cfg="`+probe.body+`"></div>`, false)
241: 		delivered := ""
242: 		for _, finding := range scan.Findings {
243: 			if finding.Rule == probe.rule {
244: 				delivered = finding.Value
245: 			}
246: 		}
247: 		if delivered != probe.password {
248: 			t.Errorf("%s delivered %q for the body %q; want the password %q, "+
249: 				"and %q is the username, which is not what was asked for",
250: 				probe.rule, delivered, probe.body, probe.password, probe.username)
251: 		}
252: 	}
253: }

260: // Three guards that the first version of this file could not prove. Each one was
261: // found by a mutation that stayed green, which is the only honest way to find a
262: // guard nobody is testing: the mutation is the question "is this line load-
263: // bearing?", and green means nobody has ever depended on it.

265: // TestCharacterRequirementsRejectThroughTheScanner isolates the requirement check
266: // from the entropy check. `kingfisher.algolia.2` is the right rule for it: it
267: // carries `min_digits: 2` and `min_entropy: 2.0`, so ten distinct letters fail
268: // the requirement while clearing the entropy floor comfortably. Without the
269: // requirement check, `algolia ABCDEFGHIJ` would be reported as a key.
270: func TestCharacterRequirementsRejectThroughTheScanner(t *testing.T) {
271: 	body := `algolia ABCDEFGHIJ`
272: 	scan := ScanSecrets(body, true)
273: 	for _, f := range scan.Findings {
274: 		if f.Rule == "kingfisher.algolia.2" {
275: 			t.Fatalf("a value with no digits was accepted: %+v", f)
276: 		}
277: 	}
278: 	found := false
279: 	for _, r := range scan.Rejected {
280: 		if r.Rule == "kingfisher.algolia.2" && strings.Contains(r.Reason, "digits") {
281: 			found = true
282: 		}
283: 	}
284: 	if !found {
285: 		t.Fatalf("expected a rejection naming the digit requirement, got %+v", scan.Rejected)
286: 	}
287: }
288: 
289: // TestEntropyRejectsThroughTheScanner isolates entropy from the character
290: // requirements. `kingfisher.airtable.2` has `min_entropy: 3.5` and no character
291: // requirements at all, so nothing but entropy can reject the value.
292: func TestEntropyRejectsThroughTheScanner(t *testing.T) {
293: 	low := ScanSecrets(`key AAAA.v1.AAAA.aaaa`, true)
294: 	for _, f := range low.Findings {
295: 		if f.Rule == "kingfisher.airtable.2" {
296: 			t.Fatalf("a low-entropy value was accepted: %+v", f)
297: 		}
298: 	}
299: 	found := false
300: 	for _, r := range low.Rejected {
301: 		if r.Rule == "kingfisher.airtable.2" && strings.Contains(r.Reason, "entropy") {
302: 			found = true
303: 		}
304: 	}
305: 	if !found {
306: 		t.Fatalf("expected an entropy rejection, got %+v", low.Rejected)
307: 	}
308: 	// And the same rule must still accept a value that clears the floor, so the
309: 	// test is about entropy rather than about the rule being broken.
310: 	high := ScanSecrets(`key KX7QP2M9.v1.A1B2C3D4.9f8e7d6c`, true)
311: 	accepted := false
312: 	for _, f := range high.Findings {
313: 		if f.Rule == "kingfisher.airtable.2" {
314: 			accepted = true
315: 		}
316: 	}
317: 	if !accepted {
318: 		t.Fatalf("a high-entropy value must be accepted; rejected=%+v", high.Rejected)
319: 	}
320: }
321: 
322: // TestEmptyCaptureGroupIsRejected covers the guard no upstream rule can reach.
323: // A rule whose group is optional matches while capturing nothing; reporting it
324: // as a finding with an empty value is worse than not reporting it, because the
325: // count would claim a credential that is not in the text.
326: func TestEmptyCaptureGroupIsRejected(t *testing.T) {
327: 	rules := []SecretRule{{
328: 		ID:    "test.optional.1",
329: 		Name:  "optional group",
330: 		Regex: regexp.MustCompile(`token(?P<secret>[A-Za-z0-9]{4})?`),
331: 		Group: 1,
332: 		Score: 50,
333: 	}}
334: 	withValue := scanWithRules("tokenAb3d", rules, true)
335: 	if len(withValue.Findings) != 1 || withValue.Findings[0].Value != "Ab3d" {
336: 		t.Fatalf("a rule with a participating group must report the value, got %+v", withValue)
337: 	}
338: 	withoutValue := scanWithRules("token", rules, true)
339: 	if len(withoutValue.Findings) != 0 {
340: 		t.Fatalf("a match with an empty capture group must not be reported: %+v", withoutValue.Findings)
341: 	}
342: 	if len(withoutValue.Rejected) != 1 ||
343: 		!strings.Contains(withoutValue.Rejected[0].Reason, "capture group") {
344: 		t.Fatalf("the empty capture must be rejected with a reason, got %+v", withoutValue.Rejected)
345: 	}
346: }

  "newString": "// scanWithRules is the body of ScanSecrets with the rule set injected.\n//\n// It exists because one guard — \"the capture group was empty\" — cannot be\n// reached by any of the 539 upstream rules: every one of them declares a group\n// that participates whenever the pattern matches. A guard that cannot be\n// reached is a guard that cannot be tested, and an untested guard is the kind of\n// line that is quietly deleted and quietly needed. Injecting the set makes the\n// branch testable without inventing a fake rule in the shipped table.\n//\n// # A rule runs on the whole body, not on one line at a time\n//\n// This is the second version, and the reason for it is measured. The first\n// version did `strings.Split(text, \"\\n\")` and ran every rule on every line\n// separately. That made every rule whose body contains `\\s`, `[\\n\\r]`, `(?s)` or\n// `(?is)` structurally unreachable, because a newline can never occur inside one\n// line — the class it needs to match was cut out of the input before the rule\n// ever saw it.\n//\n// Measured on this build: **2 of 543 rules** matched a real body when given the\n// whole text and matched zero lines of it:\n//\n//   - `np.pem.1` `PEM-Encoded Private Key`, upstream `base_score` 92\n//   - `kingfisher.privkey.2` `Contains Private Key`\n//\n// So a page serving a PEM-encoded private key reported nothing at all — not a\n// shortened value, not a rejected match, simply zero findings for the highest\n// scoring rule in the set. `-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY----- … -----END RSA PRIVATE\n// KEY-----`, and the same for the `EC`, `OPENSSH`, `PKCS8` and `PGP` headers,\n// all returned `findings=0 rejected=0`. A rule that is present, scored 92, and\n// can never fire is worse than an absent rule, because the rule count says 534\n// rules ran.\n//\n// 307 of the 543 rules carry a newline-capable class. Only two are unreachable\n// today, and that is luck rather than design: nothing stops the next rule from\n// being the third.\n//\n// The line number is therefore computed from the match offset instead of from\n// the loop variable, so the report still points at the line the analyst reads in\n// their editor. `lineAt` counts newlines before the match; it is 1-based, which\n// is the same convention `Match` uses for pattern hits.\nfunc scanWithRules(text string, rules []SecretRule, includeNoisy bool) SecretScan {\n\tscan := SecretScan{RulesAvailable: len(rules)}\n\tif strings.TrimSpace(text) == \"\" {\n\t\treturn scan\n\t}\n\tbyFingerprint := map[string]int{}\n\n\tfor _, rule := range rules {\n\t\tif rule.Noisy && !includeNoisy {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif rule.Regex == nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tscan.RulesRun++\n\t\tgroup := credentialGroup(rule)\n\t\t// The indexed form is what makes the line number possible: a match carries\n\t\t// its byte span, and the span is what maps back to a line. The indexed\n\t\t// slice is laid out as [start0, end0, start1, end1, …] over the groups, so\n\t\t// group n occupies the pair at 2n and 2n+1, and -1 marks a group that did\n\t\t// not participate.\n\t\tfor _, span := range rule.Regex.FindAllStringSubmatchIndex(text, -1) {\n\t\t\tline := lineAt(text, matchStart(span))\n\t\t\tvalue := capturedValue(span, group, text)\n\t\t\tif value == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tscan.Rejected = append(scan.Rejected, RejectedMatch{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Reason: \"no capture group held a value\", Line: line,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rule.Reject != nil && rule.Reject.MatchString(value) {\n\t\t\t\treason := rule.RejectReason\n\t\t\t\tif reason == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\t\treason = \"value matches the rule's exclusion expression\"\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tscan.Rejected = append(scan.Rejected, RejectedMatch{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Reason: reason, Line: line,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif ok, reason := rule.Requirement.Satisfied(value); !ok {\n\t\t\t\tscan.Rejected = append(scan.Rejected, RejectedMatch{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Reason: reason, Line: line,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rule.MinEntropy > 0 && shannonEntropy(value) < rule.MinEntropy {\n\t\t\t\tscan.Rejected = append(scan.Rejected, RejectedMatch{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID,\n\t\t\t\t\tReason: \"entropy \" + trimFloat(shannonEntropy(value)) +\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\" below required \" + trimFloat(rule.MinEntropy),\n\t\t\t\t\tLine: line,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif isPlaceholderValue(value) {\n\t\t\t\tscan.Rejected = append(scan.Rejected, RejectedMatch{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Reason: \"value is a documented placeholder\", Line: line,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tfingerprint := secretFingerprint(value)\n\t\t\t// One value is one finding, and the rule that reports it is the one\n\t\t\t// that scores it highest — not the one that happens to come first in\n\t\t\t// the registry. The table is 543 rules long and its order is upstream's,\n\t\t\t// so \"first\" is an accident of the port: `np.aws.6` at 95 and a project\n\t\t\t// rule at 52 both match `aws_access_key = … ; aws_secret = …`, and a\n\t\t\t// report that named the weaker claim is a report that understates the\n\t\t\t// finding. When the incoming rule outscores the recorded one the row is\n\t\t\t// replaced in place, so the stronger claim wins and the count stays at\n\t\t\t// one value.\n\t\t\tif existing, ok := byFingerprint[fingerprint]; ok {\n\t\t\t\tif scan.Findings[existing].Score >= rule.Score {\n\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tscan.Findings[existing] = SecretFinding{\n\t\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Name: rule.Name, Value: value,\n\t\t\t\t\tFingerprint: fingerprint, Score: rule.Score,\n\t\t\t\t\tSeverity: secretSeverity(rule.Score), Line: line,\n\t\t\t\t\tCategories: rule.Categories, Noisy: rule.Noisy,\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tbyFingerprint[fingerprint] = len(scan.Findings)\n\t\t\tscan.Findings = append(scan.Findings, SecretFinding{\n\t\t\t\tRule: rule.ID, Name: rule.Name, Value: value,\n\t\t\t\tFingerprint: fingerprint, Score: rule.Score,\n\t\t\t\tSeverity: secretSeverity(rule.Score), Line: line,\n\t\t\t\tCategories: rule.Categories, Noisy: rule.Noisy,\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t}"
}

{

  "newString": "// capturedValue reads the rule's capture group out of an indexed match, falling\n// back to the whole match only when the rule declares group 0.\n//\n// The indexed slice is laid out as [start0, end0, start1, end1, …]: pair n covers\n// group n, and a group that did not participate carries -1 in both slots. A group\n// that did not participate is not an empty string, it is an absent slice, and\n// conflating the two is how a rule that matched nothing gets reported as a finding\n// with no value.\nfunc capturedValue(span []int, group int, text string) string {\n\tif group <= 0 {\n\t\tif len(span) < 2 {\n\t\t\treturn \"\"\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn text[span[0]:span[1]]\n\t}\n\tpair := 2 * group\n\tif pair+1 >= len(span) {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tstart, end := span[pair], span[pair+1]\n\tif start < 0 || end < 0 {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\treturn text[start:end]\n}\n\n// matchStart is the byte offset the whole match begins at, which is what the line\n// number is computed from.\nfunc matchStart(span []int) int {\n\tif len(span) < 2 {\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\tif span[0] < 0 {\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\treturn span[0]\n}\n\n// lineAt is the 1-based line number of a byte offset in text.\n//\n// It counts the newlines before the offset rather than splitting the body into\n// lines first, because the rule now sees the whole body: the split that the line\n// number would need is the very thing that made multi-line rules unreachable.\n// A body with no newline at all is line 1, and an offset past the end still names\n// the last real line rather than a line that does not exist.\nfunc lineAt(text string, offset int) int {\n\tif offset <= 0 {\n\t\treturn 1\n\t}\n\tif offset > len(text) {\n\t\toffset = len(text)\n\t}\n\treturn 1 + strings.Count(text[:offset], \"\\n\")\n}",
  "oldString": "// capturedValue reads the rule's capture group, falling back to the whole match\n// only when the rule declares group 0.\nfunc capturedValue(match []string, group int) string {\n\tif group <= 0 {\n\t\tif len(match) > 0 {\n\t\t\treturn match[0]\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tif group < len(match) {\n\t\treturn match[group]\n\t}\n\treturn \"\"\n}"
}

The\n// list below is the whole default, it is caller-overridable, and nothing\n// truncates it unless the caller asks for a bound.\nconst secretScanDefaultDelay = 250 * time.Millisecond\n\n// secretScanDefaultBytes is how much of a body a run reads when the caller names\n// no limit.\n//\n// It is a **default, not a cap**: the caller overrides it with `max_bytes`, and\n// zero there means the whole body.
